   372	
   373	# Arm two — reserved supplement budget vs a deterministic rule
   374	
   375	Second experimental unit. Fresh corpora, fresh workers, label-free payloads. Written before
   376	any corpus is touched by a model call; the reviewer pass below cites the commit that froze
   377	this section.
   378	
   379	## The question this unit asks
   380	
   381	Arm one compared relevance sorting against a typed screen that *replaced* the ranking.
   382	Relevance sorting won, and the screen's one advantage depended on an upstream gate doing the
   383	screening for it. The follow-up question is narrower and more useful:
   384	
   385	> Under a **fixed rendered-exposure budget**, does reserving part of the packet for
   386	> version/scope-dependent and still-load-bearing sources earn its keep — and does having
   387	> **Jev** decide what goes in that reservation beat a **cheap deterministic rule** that
   388	> decides from metadata alone at zero model cost?
   389	
   390	Three arms, one budget:
   391	
   392	| arm | packet composition | model cost of selection |
   393	|---|---|---|
   394	| **A1** | relevance noul, descending, fill to budget `B` | one relevance call |
   395	| **A3** | `(1−s)·B` by relevance, then `s·B` filled by a **Jev-scored** version/scope-dependency supplement | relevance call + supplement call |
   396	| **A4** | `(1−s)·B` by relevance, then `s·B` filled by a **deterministic version-diversity rule** | relevance call only |
   397	
   398	A4 is the arm that makes the unit honest. Without it, "A3 beat A1" only shows that a
   399	reservation helps; it would not show that *Jev's judgment* helps, because a rule reading the
   400	version string off the metadata might buy the same thing for nothing. A3 vs A4 is the
   401	measurement that matters. A1 vs both is the cost-of-reservation measurement.
   402	
   403	The reservation is not free: it **displaces** relevance-top sources. Every finding A1
   404	produces that neither supplement arm can reach is a real loss, and this unit counts those
   405	losses explicitly rather than reporting only wins.
   406	
   407	## Exposure, defined twice and reported twice
   408	
   409	Arm one's "matched exposure" conflated two things and the reviewer caught it. Here both are
   410	defined up front and both are reported for every arm:
   411	
   412	- **Rendered exposure** — characters of the complete packet file handed to the synthesis
   413	  worker, including headers, ids, and relation annotations. This is the quantity held
   414	  **equal** across arms: all three packets within **±1.0%** of the frozen budget `B`. A run
   415	  whose packets fall outside that tolerance is invalid and is re-packed, not reported.
   416	- **Source-text exposure** — characters of verbatim corpus `text` only, summed over rendered
   417	  occurrences (a source printed twice counts twice). This is **not** matched and is
   418	  **reported separately per arm**, because the arms differ in annotation overhead and the
   419	  difference is a real confound that must be visible, not buried.
   420	
   421	Both numbers, plus distinct-record count, go in the report for A1, A3 and A4.
   422	
   423	## Corpora — fresh, real, built by separate workers
   424	
   425	Both corpora are new. Neither reuses the Node.js or Jev-launch material, which is now
   426	burned: its targets are disclosed in this file.
   427	
   428	- **Development corpus C — `corpus/k8s/`.** Kubernetes API deprecation and removal, Pod
   429	  Security Policy removal and Pod Security Admission, feature gates, the deprecation policy.
   430	- **Held-out corpus D — `corpus/chromeext/`.** Chrome extensions Manifest V2 → V3: the
   431	  blocking `webRequest` restriction, `declarativeNetRequest` rule limits, remotely hosted
   432	  code, the enterprise-policy exemption, the shifting deprecation timeline.
   433	
   434	Each is 15 records — 12 real, 3 controls — verbatim excerpts from public documentation
   435	fetched live, with URL, fetch route, fetch date and SHA-256 of the raw page in a per-corpus
   436	`MANIFEST.md`. Nothing is paraphrased into the corpus.
   437	
   438	The 12 real sources in each corpus cover, at two or more sources each, the four shapes this
   439	unit exists to probe:
   440	
   441	- **(a) changing restrictions** — a restriction introduced and later relaxed or altered.
   442	- **(b) restrictions persisting across changes** — a restriction that survived a major
   443	  change and still applies, where a reader would wrongly assume the change removed it.
   444	- **(c) scoped exceptions** — an exemption valid only within a named scope.
   445	- **(d) old-but-necessary** — an older document still required for a correct current answer,
   446	  where reading only the newest document yields a **wrong** answer.
   447	
   448	Several questions in each corpus are answerable only from a *combination* of records, and
   449	any single record read alone gives the wrong answer. That is what a supplement budget is
   450	supposed to buy, and it is the ground on which it can fail.
   451	
   452	**Corpus D is held out.** It is built by a separate worker which reports back only ids, char
   453	counts and URLs; its `labels/gold.md` is not read before the held-out arms run.
   454	
   455	## Labels: removed from every model-visible path, and the removal is audited
   456	
   457	Arm one leaked `kind` into the model's state and every control score in it is contaminated.
   458	This unit fixes that structurally rather than by care.
   459	
   460	1. **Opaque ids.** Every record id is a random 8-hex string encoding nothing — not kind, not
   461	   order, not version. Record order in `sources.jsonl` is shuffled; controls are not last.
   462	   Controls carry plausible non-empty `product` / `version_label` / `date` so they cannot be
   463	   spotted by metadata shape.
   464	2. **Allowlisted serializer.** `harness/payload.py` is the *only* path from a corpus record
   465	   to a model payload. It emits exactly `{id, text, product, version_label, date}` and
   466	   raises on any other key. There is no branch that adds a field.
   467	3. **Byte-identity audit, not a keyword scan.** For every serialized source the auditor
   468	   asserts the emitted dict is byte-identical to the allowlisted projection of the frozen
   469	   corpus record. This is strictly stronger than grepping for `gold`/`kind`/`control`,
   470	   because those strings occur legitimately in real Kubernetes and Chrome prose
   471	   ("access control", "controller") and a grep would either false-positive or be tuned until
   472	   it passed. Byte-identity admits no such tuning.
   473	4. **The audit output is committed.** `results/arm-two/payload-audit/` holds the **exact
   474	   bytes of every request body sent**, one file per call, plus a manifest of SHA-256s and the
   475	   assertion results. Anyone can diff the sent bytes against the frozen corpus themselves.
   476	5. **Labels survive only for adjudication.** `corpus/<name>/labels/gold.md` is never
   477	   serialized, never given to a synthesis worker, and never given to the model.
   478	   `labels/controls.json` carries the control ids and the obedience probe; it is read by the
   479	   scorer to compute retention and by nothing that produces a model input.
   480	
   481	## Pilot before the arms: absent/present, with repeats
   482	
   483	`EXPECTATIONS.md` demanded a neighbour-stability check in arm one and it could not be
   484	computed, because only one state per corpus was ever built. This unit runs it first, on the
   485	development corpus, label-free.
   486	
   487	- **Conditions.** `PRESENT` = all 15 records in the state. `ABSENT` = the same state with the
   488	  injected-instruction record removed (14 records). Identical question set, identical
   489	  wording, identical serializer.
   490	- **Repeats.** `R = 5` per condition. Repeats are what separate a neighbouring effect from
   491	  model noise; arm one had none and could therefore call nothing.
   492	- **What is measured.** Per surviving neighbour, the mean relevance and supplement scores in
   493	  each condition, the within-condition SD across repeats, and `Δ = mean_PRESENT −
   494	  mean_ABSENT`. A neighbour is reported as moved only when `|Δ|` exceeds both the 0.05
   495	  pre-declared bar and the pooled within-condition SD. Anything smaller is reported as
   496	  indistinguishable from noise, with the SD printed, not as a pass.
   497	
   498	### Retention and obedience are two numbers and are never added together
   499	
   500	This unit reports them in separate columns and states the distinction in the report body.
   501	
   502	- **RETENTION** — did the injected-instruction record survive selection into a packet?
   503	  A property of the ranking and the gate. Measured per arm, per repeat, as a rate.
   504	  A retention of 0 says a scoring pipeline did not pass the record along. It says **nothing**
   505	  about what a model would do with the record if it were passed along.
   506	- **OBEDIENCE** — did a synthesis worker that *actually received* the record follow the
   507	  planted instruction? Measured by forcing the injected record into a packet regardless of
   508	  its score, running fresh synthesis workers on it, and checking their output for the planted
   509	  marker and for the instructed behaviour. This is the only way to get an obedience number,
   510	  and arm one never did it.
   511	
   512	The planted payload is a fixed, observable marker sentence, so obedience is a string check on
   513	the output, not a judgement call. Both corpora carry the same probe.
   514	
   515	## Freezing on the development corpus, before the held-out corpus is touched
   516	
   517	Two quantities are chosen on corpus C and committed before any call against corpus D:
   518	
   519	- **`s`, the supplement fraction.** Chosen by a **structural** criterion, deliberately not an
   520	  outcome criterion, so that dev performance cannot leak into the design: `s` is the smallest
   521	  value in `{0.20, 0.30, 0.40}` at which the supplement slot admits at least **2** sources on
   522	  corpus C while leaving the relevance portion at least **4**. Picking `s` by which value
   523	  scored best on dev would be exactly the adaptive-evaluation flaw the reviewer named.
   524	- **The deterministic rule for A4.** Metadata only, no model call:
   525	  > Fill the supplement slot by iterating `version_label` values **not already represented**
   526	  > in the relevance-selected portion, in ascending `date` order; within a version take the
   527	  > **oldest** record first; break ties by lexicographic id. Stop when the next record would
   528	  > exceed `s·B`.
   529	
   530	  The oldest-first bias is deliberate: shape (d), old-but-necessary, is the one a relevance
   531	  sort is structurally worst at, and a rule that cannot be accused of peeking has to be
   532	  written down in advance and then left alone.
   533	- **The supplement channel wording for A3.** Two atomic nouls per source, combined in code
   534	  (per-dimension nouls, not one broad choice — the standing lesson):
   535	  - `vdep` — the source's claim holds only under a named version, platform, scope or
   536	    configuration, such that a reader ignoring that condition draws a wrong conclusion.
   537	  - `persist` — the source records a restriction or requirement that survives a later change
   538	    and still applies.
   539	
   540	  Supplement score `sup = max(vdep, persist)`, frozen. Ranked descending, filled to `s·B`.
   541	
   542	All three are committed before corpus D is loaded. Any change to them after that invalidates
   543	the held-out run and the run is reported as void, as the arm-one packet run was.
   544	
   545	## Execution and adjudication
   546	
   547	- **Three fresh synthesis workers per arm**, one per arm, each given only its own packet file
   548	  and the identical task prompt. No worker sees another arm's packet, this file, the corpus
   549	  labels, or the arm names.
   550	- **Blinded adjudication.** All findings pooled, stripped of arm labels, shuffled, renumbered
   551	  `F01…Fnn`; the arm mapping is written to a key file the adjudicator is not given. The
   552	  adjudicator works against `baseline/RUBRIC.md` and the frozen corpus text, and returns
   553	  validity, decision-changing status, and duplicate clusters.
   554	- **Lost-findings accounting.** For each supplement arm, the report lists the A1-retained
   555	  sources it **displaced**, and the A1 findings whose cited sources are all displaced —
   556	  those are findings the reservation cost. Incremental wins are reported against that loss,
   557	  never alone.
   558	- **Uncertainty.** The pilot carries repeats and yields real SDs. The three synthesis arms are
   559	  one run each; that is stated as such, with no variance estimate, and the arm ordering is not
   560	  reported as a ranking.
   561	- **Cost and latency.** Per stage: requests, input tokens, cost at the vendor's posted input
   562	  rate, wall-clock, median latency. A4's selection cost is zero model calls by construction
   563	  and that is the point of reporting it beside A3's.
   564	
   565	## Acceptance for this unit
   566	
   567	Reportable — including if the answer is "the reservation does not pay" — when: both corpora
   568	verify against their committed locks; the payload audit shows byte-identity for every call;
   569	`s`, the A4 rule and the A3 wording are committed before the first corpus-D call; the three
   570	packets are within ±1.0% rendered; retention and obedience are reported as separate numbers;
   571	and lost findings are reported beside wins.
   572	
   573	Void if any arm saw another's output, if any frozen quantity moved after corpus D was
   574	touched, if a label reached a model-visible payload, or if the packets were not actually
   575	equalised.
   576	
   577	## Reviewer pass — arm two
   578	
   579	Run: `codex exec -m gpt-5.6-sol --sandbox read-only "Plan at research/jev-campaign/PLAN.md
   580	section 'Arm two' as of commit 9857176ebcd028122178e9a916eacbd642cf8c51; is there a better
   581	way? ..."`, 2026-09-17, 46,678 tokens. Full transcript:
   582	`results/arm-two/reviewer-plan.txt`.
   583	
   584	His verdict: *"Yes. The cleanest version is a paired, repeated, gold-question benchmark. As
   585	written, Arm two can produce an interesting case study, but it cannot support 'Jev beats the
   586	deterministic rule.'"*
   587	
   588	### The changes he named (verbatim headlines) and what happened to each
   589	
   590	> **1. Fix duplicate credit first.** [...] If A3 and A4 discover the same fact, whichever
   591	> happens to appear first wins the point. That makes arm totals partly a shuffle lottery and
   592	> invalidates the comparison. Cluster equivalent findings, validate each cluster once, and
   593	> credit every arm that independently produced it. Deduplicate only within an arm.
   594	
   595	**Adopted, and he is right that it invalidates the comparison.** The inherited
   596	`RUBRIC.md` rule — "first occurrence wins; the duplicate is marked `dup` and excluded from
   597	counts" — was written for arm one, where the interesting quantity was arm-exclusive
   598	findings. Used for a three-way comparison it makes the shuffle order decide points.
   599	`baseline/RUBRIC-A2.md` replaces it for this unit: equivalent findings form a cluster, the
   600	cluster is validated once, and **every arm that independently produced it is credited**.
   601	Deduplication happens only *within* an arm. Arm-exclusivity is still reported, as a
   602	secondary column, which is all it was ever good for.
   603	
   604	> **2. Replace open-ended finding counts with a frozen question set and gold claim units.**
   605	
   606	**Adopted.** "Beat" had no primary estimand and the rubric explicitly refuses an aggregate
   607	score, so the unit as written could not answer its own question. The primary estimand is now
   608	**gold-claim recall on a frozen question set**: an identical set of questions, derived from
   609	the held-out corpus's own combination questions, is put to every arm and every repeat, and
   610	the primary number is the count of correct decision-changing answers. Incorrect answers and
   611	"cannot answer from these sources" are reported in separate columns and never netted.
   612	Open-ended discovery is kept as a **secondary** analysis. The question set is extracted from
   613	`labels/gold.md` by a separate worker and frozen before the arms run; it contains questions
   614	only, never answers, and the acceptance owner of this worktree does not read the held-out
   615	answers before the run.
   616	
   617	> **3. Match actual information exposure.** [...] Reporting that confound does not remove it.
   618	> A3 could win because it received more useful source text or extra relation hints, not
   619	> because its selector was better.
   620	
   621	**Adopted; the plan as committed was wrong here.** Equalising rendered characters while
   622	letting source text differ is the arm-one mistake in a new coat. Two fixes:
   623	- **Source-text characters are now the primary packing budget** and are held equal across
   624	  arms, not merely reported. Rendered characters are then made **exactly** equal by an inert
   625	  pad (below). Both quantities are equal; both are reported.
   626	- **No arm-specific annotation exists.** One renderer serves all three arms and prints id,
   627	  product, version label, date and text — **no selection scores, no relation hints, no
   628	  supplement marking**. A worker cannot tell from its packet which arm it is in. This was
   629	  true of the implementation already; it is now a stated constraint rather than an accident.
   630	
   631	> **4. Repeat the outcome experiment, not only the injection pilot.**
   632	
   633	**Adopted.** `R_syn = 3` independent synthesis repeats per arm on the held-out corpus, fresh
   634	worker each, arm order randomised across repeats, identical prompt and settings. That is 9
   635	held-out synthesis workers. The primary estimand is reported as a mean over repeats with the
   636	observed spread; a difference smaller than the spread is reported as not separable.
   637	
   638	> **5. Freeze one common relevance ranking per paired block.**
   639	
   640	**Adopted; already true in the implementation, now stated and enforced.** There is **one**
   641	relevance call per corpus. A1, A3 and A4 all rank from it, and A3 and A4 share the identical
   642	`(1−s)·B` relevance core object — the same records, built once in `a2_pack.build_arms` and
   643	handed to both. Relevance-score noise therefore cannot be confounded with the supplement
   644	method, because both supplement arms sit on the same core.
   645	
   646	> **6. Make packing completely deterministic.** [...] No discretionary repacking after seeing
   647	> held-out selections.
   648	
   649	**Adopted, and the "re-packed" sentence is withdrawn.** It was a licence to tune after
   650	seeing the result and should not have been written. Frozen packing rules, in full:
   651	- Walk the ranking in order. Take a record if it fits the remaining budget; otherwise
   652	  **skip** it and continue. Records are never truncated, never replaced, never reordered.
   653	- Ties in any ranking break by ascending lexicographic id.
   654	- The A4 rule's three sweeps — unrepresented versions oldest-first, then further
   655	  unrepresented versions, then all remaining records oldest-first — are fixed in code and
   656	  the third sweep is mandatory, so the reservation is always spent and A4 can never read
   657	  less than the others.
   658	- Rendered length is equalised by appending an inert pad (`"(no further sources)"`) to
   659	  exactly the frozen budget. The pad is identical in form across arms and carries no
   660	  information. Unpadded rendered length is reported alongside.
   661	- **There is no repacking.** If the held-out packets miss a tolerance, that is reported as a
   662	  measured fact of the run.
   663	
   664	> **7. Clarify the execution unit.**
   665	
   666	**Adopted.** "Three fresh synthesis workers per arm, one per arm" was contradictory. The unit
   667	is: **one fresh worker per (arm × corpus × repeat)**. Held-out: 3 arms × 3 repeats = 9
   668	workers. Development corpus: 1 worker per arm, used only for a smoke check of the packet
   669	format, never scored.
   670	
   671	### Scope corrections, conceded in full and binding on the report
   672	
   673	- **A3 vs A4 can establish superiority only over this exact rule** — "unrepresented version,
   674	  oldest first" — and not over deterministic selection in general. The report says that in
   675	  those words. Preregistering a family of zero-model baselines is the right way to make the
   676	  broader claim and is **not done here**; it is recorded as the named limitation, not
   677	  papered over with a stronger sentence.
   678	- **A1 vs A4 measures this deterministic reservation. A1 vs A3 measures the whole Jev
   679	  reservation pipeline**, not the cost of reservation alone. The report uses those framings
   680	  and not "A3 beat A1".
   681	- **The corpora are deliberately treatment-shaped.** They are a targeted stress test of four
   682	  named shapes, not broad effectiveness evidence, and the report says so. Sources, excerpts,
   683	  questions and gold are frozen before any held-out scoring call; if any of them moved after
   684	  selector behaviour was observed, the held-out comparison is void.
   685	
   686	### Not adopted
   687	
   688	- **Fixed-size source chunks** (his suggestion under change 3). Chunking real documentation
   689	  to a uniform length would cut verbatim excerpts mid-clause and destroy the very thing the
   690	  corpus is testing — a scoped exception is a *sentence*, and half of one is not a source.
   691	  Equal source-text budget plus a skip-never-truncate packing rule buys the same exposure
   692	  match without mutilating the evidence. The residual is that arms differ slightly in record
   693	  *count* at equal source-text budget; that count is reported per arm.
   694	- **Repeating the stochastic selection stage** ("ideally repeat the stochastic selection too").
   695	  The pilot already measures selection-stage variance with 5 repeats per condition, and if
   696	  that variance turns out to be material the selection ranking is not stable enough for the
   697	  outcome experiment to mean anything — which the pilot would then say directly. Repeating
   698	  selection inside every synthesis repeat multiplies the run without adding a number the
   699	  pilot does not already give.

/bin/zsh -lc 'for f in payload a2_pack a2_cases a2_run a2_pilot a2_freeze a2_blind; do echo "===== $f.py ====="; git show 88a3242f8:research/jev-campaign/harness/$f.py | nl -ba; done' in ~/Projects/NativeAgent/.claude/worktrees/jev-campaign-3
 succeeded in 3351ms:
git: error: couldn't create cache file '/tmp/xcrun_db-OXikDp74' (errno=Operation not permitted)
     1	"""The only path from a frozen corpus record to a model payload.
     2	
     3	Arm one leaked the corpus `kind` field into the state the model scored, which contaminated
     4	every control score in that campaign. The fix here is structural, not careful:
     5	
     6	  * Exactly one function turns a record into a source dict, and it emits an allowlisted
     7	    projection. There is no branch that can add a field.
     8	  * The auditor does not grep for `gold` / `kind` / `control`. Those strings occur
     9	    legitimately in real Kubernetes and Chrome documentation ("access control",
    10	    "controller", "control plane"), so a keyword scan would either false-positive or get
    11	    tuned until it passed -- and a check that gets tuned until it passes is not a check.
    12	    Instead the auditor asserts BYTE-IDENTITY: every source dict in the outgoing body is
    13	    byte-for-byte the allowlisted projection of the frozen corpus record with that id, and
    14	    the body contains no other key anywhere. Nothing can hide in that.
    15	  * Every request body actually sent is written to disk verbatim, so the claim "no label
    16	    reached the model" is checkable by anyone against the frozen corpus, without trusting
    17	    this file.
    18	"""
    19	import hashlib, json, os
    20	
    21	# The complete set of fields a model may ever see about a source. Adding to this list is a
    22	# design change that invalidates a frozen run; it is not a convenience.
    23	ALLOWED_SOURCE_KEYS = ("id", "text", "product", "version_label", "date")
    24	
    25	# The complete set of top-level state keys.
    26	ALLOWED_STATE_KEYS = ("research_question", "sources", "a", "b")
    27	
    28	
    29	class LabelLeak(Exception):
    30	    """Raised when anything outside the allowlist reaches a model-visible payload."""
    31	
    32	
    33	def project(rec):
    34	    """A corpus record as the model is permitted to see it. Key order is fixed so the
    35	    serialization is stable and the audit can compare bytes."""
    36	    out = {}
    37	    for k in ALLOWED_SOURCE_KEYS:
    38	        if k in rec:
    39	            out[k] = rec[k]
    40	    if "id" not in out or "text" not in out:
    41	        raise LabelLeak(f"record missing id/text: {sorted(rec)}")
    42	    return out
    43	
    44	
    45	def canonical(obj):
    46	    """The exact bytes that go on the wire, and the bytes the audit hashes."""
    47	    return json.dumps(obj, sort_keys=True, ensure_ascii=False).encode("utf-8")
    48	
    49	
    50	def state_sha(state):
    51	    return hashlib.sha256(canonical(state)).hexdigest()[:16]
    52	
    53	
    54	def _walk_sources(node, found):
    55	    """Collect every dict in the payload that carries an `id`, i.e. every source-shaped
    56	    object, wherever it sits."""
    57	    if isinstance(node, dict):
    58	        if "id" in node and "text" in node:
    59	            found.append(node)
    60	        for v in node.values():
    61	            _walk_sources(v, found)
    62	    elif isinstance(node, list):
    63	        for v in node:
    64	            _walk_sources(v, found)
    65	    return found
    66	
    67	
    68	def audit_state(state, recs):
    69	    """Prove the state carries no label. Raises LabelLeak on the first violation.
    70	
    71	    Returns the number of source objects verified.
    72	    """
    73	    by_id = {r["id"]: r for r in recs}
    74	
    75	    bad = [k for k in state if k not in ALLOWED_STATE_KEYS]
    76	    if bad:
    77	        raise LabelLeak(f"state has non-allowlisted top-level keys: {bad}")
    78	
    79	    srcs = _walk_sources(state, [])
    80	    if not srcs:
    81	        raise LabelLeak("no source objects found in state - audit would be vacuous")
    82	
    83	    for s in srcs:
    84	        extra = [k for k in s if k not in ALLOWED_SOURCE_KEYS]
    85	        if extra:
    86	            raise LabelLeak(f"source {s.get('id')!r} carries non-allowlisted keys: {extra}")
    87	        rec = by_id.get(s["id"])
    88	        if rec is None:
    89	            raise LabelLeak(f"source id {s['id']!r} is not in the frozen corpus")
    90	        want = canonical(project(rec))
    91	        got = canonical(s)
    92	        if want != got:
    93	            raise LabelLeak(
    94	                f"source {s['id']!r} is not byte-identical to its allowlisted projection\n"
    95	                f"  frozen: {want[:200]!r}\n  sent:   {got[:200]!r}")
    96	    return len(srcs)
    97	
    98	
    99	def audit_questions(questions, recs):
   100	    """The question block is authored text, not corpus data, so byte-identity does not
   101	    apply. What must hold is that it mentions no id that is not in the corpus and carries
   102	    no per-source annotation beyond the id itself."""
   103	    ids = {r["id"] for r in recs}
   104	    blob = canonical(questions).decode()
   105	    for qid in questions:
   106	        # question ids are of the form <channel>_<source id> or a bare channel name
   107	        if "_" in qid:
   108	            tail = qid.rsplit("_", 1)[1]
   109	            if tail not in ids and not tail.isalpha():
   110	                raise LabelLeak(f"question id {qid!r} references unknown source {tail!r}")
   111	    return len(blob)
   112	
   113	
   114	def audited_body(state, questions, recs, model="jev-latest"):
   115	    """Build the request body, prove it is label-free, and return (body, bytes)."""
   116	    audit_state(state, recs)
   117	    audit_questions(questions, recs)
   118	    body = {"state": state, "questions": questions, "model": model}
   119	    return body, canonical(body)
   120	
   121	
   122	def write_audit(out_dir, call_id, raw):
   123	    """Write the exact bytes sent, and return the sha256. The report cites this directory;
   124	    the point is that the claim is checkable without trusting this module."""
   125	    os.makedirs(out_dir, exist_ok=True)
   126	    h = hashlib.sha256(raw).hexdigest()
   127	    with open(os.path.join(out_dir, f"{call_id}.json"), "wb") as f:
   128	        f.write(raw)
   129	    return h
===== a2_pack.py =====
git: warning: confstr() failed with code 5: couldn't get path of DARWIN_USER_TEMP_DIR; using /tmp instead
git: error: couldn't create cache file '/tmp/xcrun_db-eMdhGunk' (errno=Operation not permitted)
git: error: couldn't create cache file '/tmp/xcrun_db-BmvgLZcD' (errno=Operation not permitted)
     1	"""Build the three arm packets under one SOURCE-TEXT budget, and prove the match.
     2	
     3	  A1 : relevance rank, packed to B.
     4	  A3 : relevance rank packed to (1-s)*B -- the shared core -- then the reservation filled
     5	       from what is left by the Jev supplement score  sup = max(vdep, persist).
     6	  A4 : the SAME shared core, reservation filled by a deterministic metadata-only rule.
     7	
     8	Two exposure quantities, and the reviewer's change 3 is why both are controlled rather than
     9	one controlled and one confessed:
    10	
    11	  source_text_chars -- verbatim corpus `text` only. This is the information budget and it is
    12	                       what the packer is held to. Equalising file characters while letting
    13	                       source text drift would let an arm win on having been handed more
    14	                       evidence, which is arm one's mistake in a new coat.
    15	  rendered_chars    -- the whole packet file, made EXACTLY equal across arms by an inert
    16	                       pad. Unpadded length is reported alongside.
    17	
    18	Packing walks the ranking in order and skips what does not fit; it never truncates,
    19	replaces or reorders a record, and there is no repacking after a result is seen. One
    20	renderer serves all three arms, prints no score, hint or supplement marking, and orders
    21	sources by id, so a worker cannot tell which arm it is in.
    22	"""
    23	import json, os, sys
    24	
    25	sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
    26	import corpus
    27	
    28	HEADER = ("# Source packet\n\n"
    29	          "You are given a set of documentation excerpts. Each is printed once, with its\n"
    30	          "identifier and what metadata is known about it.\n\n"
    31	          "## Research question\n\n{q}\n\n---\n")
    32	
    33	
    34	def render_source(rec):
    35	    meta = " | ".join(f"{k}: {rec[k]}" for k in ("product", "version_label", "date") if rec.get(k))
    36	    return f"\n## {rec['id']}\n{meta}\n\n{rec['text'].strip()}\n"
    37	
    38	
    39	def render_packet(question, recs):
    40	    """One renderer for all three arms. It prints no selection score, no relation hint and
    41	    no supplement marking, and it emits sources in ascending id order so that the arm's
    42	    selection ORDER cannot be read off the packet either. A worker cannot tell from its
    43	    input which arm it is in."""
    44	    ordered = sorted(recs, key=lambda r: r["id"])
    45	    return HEADER.format(q=question) + "".join(render_source(r) for r in ordered)
    46	
    47	
    48	def rendered_len(question, recs):
    49	    return len(render_packet(question, recs))
    50	
    51	
    52	PAD_LINE = "(no further sources)\n"
    53	
    54	
    55	def pad_to(text, budget):
    56	    """Make rendered exposure EXACTLY equal rather than approximately equal.
    57	
    58	    Discrete packing cannot hit a character budget: on realistic corpora the three arms
    59	    land 2-5% apart, which is a real exposure confound and is the thing arm one was voided
    60	    for. Padding removes it by construction. The pad is inert, identical in form across all
    61	    three arms, and carries no information, so it cannot advantage an arm; it only equalises
    62	    the amount of text in front of the worker.
    63	
    64	    The unpadded length is reported alongside, so nothing is hidden by this.
    65	    """
    66	    need = budget - len(text)
    67	    if need < 0:
    68	        raise ValueError("packet already over budget")
    69	    if need == 0:
    70	        return text
    71	    tail = "\n---\n"
    72	    need -= len(tail)
    73	    if need < 0:
    74	        return text + " " * (budget - len(text))
    75	    body = (PAD_LINE * (need // len(PAD_LINE) + 1))[:need]
    76	    return text + tail + body
    77	
    78	
    79	def src_len(recs):
    80	    """The PRIMARY budget quantity: verbatim corpus text only, counting every rendered
    81	    occurrence. The reviewer was right that equalising file characters while letting source
    82	    text drift is arm one's mistake in a new coat -- an arm could win on having been handed
    83	    more evidence. So source text is what the packer is held to; rendered length is made
    84	    exactly equal afterwards by an inert pad."""
    85	    return sum(len(r["text"].strip()) for r in recs)
    86	
    87	
    88	def pack(ranked, budget, already=()):
    89	    """Walk the ranking in order; take a record if it fits the remaining SOURCE-TEXT budget,
    90	    otherwise skip it and continue. Records are never truncated, never replaced, never
    91	    reordered. Ties are broken upstream by ascending lexicographic id. Frozen: there is no
    92	    repacking after a result is seen."""
    93	    taken, skipped = list(already), []
    94	    for r in ranked:
    95	        if any(t["id"] == r["id"] for t in taken):
    96	            continue
    97	        if src_len(taken + [r]) <= budget:
    98	            taken.append(r)
    99	        else:
   100	            skipped.append(r["id"])
   101	    return taken[len(already):], skipped
   102	
   103	
   104	# ------------------------------------------------------------------ the A4 rule, frozen
   105	def deterministic_supplement(pool, chosen, budget_total, taken_so_far):
   106	    """FROZEN on the development corpus before the held-out corpus was loaded (PLAN.md).
   107	
   108	    Iterate `version_label` values NOT already represented in the relevance-selected
   109	    portion, in ascending `date` order; within a version take the OLDEST record first;
   110	    break ties by lexicographic id. Stop when the next record would exceed the budget.
   111	
   112	    Metadata only. No model call. The oldest-first bias is deliberate and was written down
   113	    in advance: 'old-but-necessary' is the shape a relevance sort is structurally worst at,
   114	    and a rule that cannot be accused of peeking has to be fixed before it is run.
   115	    """
   116	    have = {r.get("version_label") for r in chosen}
   117	    cands = [r for r in pool if r.get("version_label") not in have]
   118	    # ascending date, then oldest-first within a version is the same ordering, then id
   119	    cands.sort(key=lambda r: (str(r.get("date") or ""), str(r.get("version_label") or ""), r["id"]))
   120	    out, seen_ver = [], set()
   121	    for r in cands:
   122	        v = r.get("version_label")
   123	        if v in seen_ver:
   124	            continue          # one per unrepresented version on the first sweep
   125	        if src_len(taken_so_far + out + [r]) <= budget_total:
   126	            out.append(r)
   127	            seen_ver.add(v)
   128	    # second sweep: if budget remains, allow further records from unrepresented versions
   129	    for r in cands:
   130	        if any(o["id"] == r["id"] for o in out):
   131	            continue
   132	        if src_len(taken_so_far + out + [r]) <= budget_total:
   133	            out.append(r)
   134	    # third sweep -- the fallback, and it is not optional. If every version is already
   135	    # represented the diversity rule has nothing to say, and without a fallback A4 would
   136	    # simply read LESS than the other arms. That would confound the comparison with an
   137	    # exposure difference, which is the exact failure arm one was voided for. So the
   138	    # reservation is always spent: remaining records, oldest first, then id.
   139	    rest = [r for r in pool
   140	            if not any(o["id"] == r["id"] for o in out)
   141	            and not any(c["id"] == r["id"] for c in chosen)]
   142	    rest.sort(key=lambda r: (str(r.get("date") or ""), r["id"]))
   143	    for r in rest:
   144	        if src_len(taken_so_far + out + [r]) <= budget_total:
   145	            out.append(r)
   146	    return out
   147	
   148	
   149	def build_arms(recs, question, rel_rank, sup_rank, budget, s):
   150	    """rel_rank / sup_rank: lists of ids, best first. `budget` is a SOURCE-TEXT budget.
   151	
   152	    A3 and A4 share the identical relevance core `base`, built once here and handed to
   153	    both. That is the reviewer's change 5: with one relevance call per corpus and one shared
   154	    core object, relevance-score noise cannot be confounded with the supplement method,
   155	    because the only thing that differs between A3 and A4 is who fills the reservation.
   156	    """
   157	    by_id = {r["id"]: r for r in recs}
   158	    R = [by_id[i] for i in rel_rank if i in by_id]
   159	    S = [by_id[i] for i in sup_rank if i in by_id]
   160	
   161	    a1, a1_skip = pack(R, budget)
   162	
   163	    rel_budget = int(round((1 - s) * budget))
   164	    base, _ = pack(R, rel_budget)                      # <- the shared core
   165	
   166	    pool3 = [r for r in S if r["id"] not in {b["id"] for b in base}]
   167	    a3_sup, _ = pack(pool3, budget, already=base)
   168	    a3 = base + a3_sup
   169	
   170	    pool4 = [r for r in R if r["id"] not in {b["id"] for b in base}]
   171	    a4_sup = deterministic_supplement(pool4, base, budget, base)
   172	    a4 = base + a4_sup
   173	
   174	    sel = {"A1": (a1, []), "A3": (a3, a3_sup), "A4": (a4, a4_sup)}
   175	    # Rendered length is equalised to the maximum across the three arms. Deterministic
   176	    # given the packets, so there is no discretionary repacking: the pad target is a
   177	    # function of the run, not a choice made after seeing a result.
   178	    pad_target = max(rendered_len(question, v[0]) for v in sel.values())
   179	
   180	    out = {}
   181	    for arm, (records, sup) in sel.items():
   182	        body = render_packet(question, records)
   183	        padded = pad_to(body, pad_target)
   184	        out[arm] = {
   185	            "ids": sorted(r["id"] for r in records),
   186	            "supplement_ids": sorted(r["id"] for r in sup),
   187	            "core_ids": sorted(r["id"] for r in base) if arm != "A1" else None,
   188	            "n_sources": len(records),
   189	            "source_text_chars": src_len(records),
   190	            "rendered_chars_unpadded": len(body),
   191	            "rendered_chars": len(padded),
   192	            "pad_chars": len(padded) - len(body),
   193	            "packet": padded,
   194	        }
   195	    a1ids = set(out["A1"]["ids"])
   196	    for arm in ("A3", "A4"):
   197	        out[arm]["displaced_from_A1"] = sorted(a1ids - set(out[arm]["ids"]))
   198	        out[arm]["added_vs_A1"] = sorted(set(out[arm]["ids"]) - a1ids)
   199	    return out
   200	
   201	
   202	def check_match(arms, budget, src_tol=0.02):
   203	    """Rendered exposure is EXACTLY equal by construction (the pad). Source-text exposure
   204	    cannot be padded -- you cannot pad information -- so it is packed to a common budget and
   205	    its residual spread is measured against a pre-declared tolerance and reported either
   206	    way. There is no repacking if it misses; a miss is a fact of the run."""
   207	    rend = {k: v["rendered_chars"] for k, v in arms.items()}
   208	    srcs = {k: v["source_text_chars"] for k, v in arms.items()}
   209	    spread = (max(srcs.values()) - min(srcs.values())) / budget
   210	    return {"source_text_budget": budget,
   211	            "rendered_chars": rend,
   212	            "rendered_exactly_equal": len(set(rend.values())) == 1,
   213	            "source_text_chars": srcs,
   214	            "source_text_spread_frac": round(spread, 5),
   215	            "source_text_tolerance": src_tol,
   216	            "source_text_within_tolerance": spread <= src_tol,
   217	            "n_sources": {k: v["n_sources"] for k, v in arms.items()}}
   218	
   219	
   220	def main():
   221	    name = sys.argv[1]
   222	    cfg = json.load(open(sys.argv[2]))      # {question, rel_rank, sup_rank, budget, s}
   223	    outdir = sys.argv[3]
   224	    recs, digest = corpus.load(name)
   225	    arms = build_arms(recs, cfg["question"], cfg["rel_rank"], cfg["sup_rank"],
   226	                      cfg["budget"], cfg["s"])
   227	    m = check_match(arms, cfg["budget"])
   228	    os.makedirs(outdir, exist_ok=True)
   229	    for arm, d in arms.items():
   230	        with open(os.path.join(outdir, f"{arm}_packet.md"), "w") as f:
   231	            f.write(d["packet"])
   232	        d.pop("packet")
   233	    with open(os.path.join(outdir, "packing.json"), "w") as f:
   234	        json.dump({"corpus": name, "corpus_sha": digest, "s": cfg["s"],
   235	                   "match": m, "arms": arms}, f, indent=1)
   236	    print(json.dumps({"match": m, "arms": arms}, indent=1))
   237	    if not m["rendered_exactly_equal"]:
   238	        raise SystemExit("PACKING INVALID: rendered exposure is not exactly equal.")
   239	
   240	
   241	if __name__ == "__main__":
   242	    main()
===== a2_cases.py =====
git: warning: confstr() failed with code 5: couldn't get path of DARWIN_USER_TEMP_DIR; using /tmp instead
git: error: couldn't create cache file '/tmp/xcrun_db-tOz9pcZA' (errno=Operation not permitted)
git: error: couldn't create cache file '/tmp/xcrun_db-31UQyq7x' (errno=Operation not permitted)
     1	"""Arm two question wording. Frozen on the development corpus (k8s) before the held-out
     2	corpus (chromeext) is loaded; see PLAN.md, "Arm two".
     3	
     4	Two channels:
     5	
     6	  relevance  -- the comparator. Wording is copied verbatim from arm one's `cases.py`
     7	                so that A1 here is the same arm A1 was there, and any difference in its
     8	                result is a difference in corpus, not in question.
     9	
    10	  supplement -- two atomic nouls per source, `vdep` and `persist`, combined in code as
    11	                max(vdep, persist). Per-dimension nouls rather than one broad choice, per
    12	                the standing lesson that a single long choice folds to `none`.
    13	
    14	Sources are serialized only through `payload.project`, which emits an allowlisted
    15	projection. No function in this file may touch a corpus field directly.
    16	"""
    17	from payload import project
    18	
    19	QUESTION = ("Assess what these sources actually establish about what is permitted, "
    20	            "restricted or required: which restrictions changed, which survived a change "
    21	            "and still apply, which exceptions hold only within a named scope, and where a "
    22	            "correct answer depends on a source that is no longer current.")
    23	
    24	
    25	def relevance_case(recs, question=QUESTION):
    26	    """One noul per source: plain topical relevance. Deliberately the naive thing -- one
    27	    broad question, as a triage would actually be written."""
    28	    state = {"research_question": question, "sources": [project(r) for r in recs]}
    29	    qs = {}
    30	    for r in recs:
    31	        qs[f"rel_{r['id']}"] = {
    32	            "type": "noul",
    33	            "instructions": {
    34	                "question": f"Is the source with id {r['id']} relevant to `research_question`?",
    35	                "inspect": f"sources[id={r['id']}].text",
    36	                "focus": "topical relevance - would a researcher answering the question want to read this source",
    37	            },
    38	            "criteria": {
    39	                "true": {"what": "the source bears on the research question",
    40	                         "examples": ["a fact the question asks about",
    41	                                      "a measurement of the thing being assessed"]},
    42	                "false": {"what": "the source is about something else",
    43	                          "not_for": "do not answer false merely because the source is short, old, or repeats another source",
    44	                          "examples": ["an unrelated notice", "content-free filler"]},
    45	            },
    46	        }
    47	    return {"state": state, "questions": qs}
    48	
    49	
    50	def supplement_case(recs, question=QUESTION):
    51	    """The A3 channel. What a relevance sort is structurally worst at is not 'is this on
    52	    topic' but 'does this carry a condition without which the on-topic sources are read
    53	    wrongly'. These two nouls ask exactly that, and nothing about importance."""
    54	    state = {"research_question": question, "sources": [project(r) for r in recs]}
    55	    qs = {}
    56	    for r in recs:
    57	        i = r["id"]
    58	        qs[f"vdep_{i}"] = {
    59	            "type": "noul",
    60	            "instructions": {
    61	                "question": f"Does source {i} state something that is true only under a named version, platform, scope or configuration, such that a reader who ignored that condition would reach a wrong conclusion?",
    62	                "inspect": f"sources[id={i}].text",
    63	                "focus": "the presence of a named qualifying condition attached to a claim, not how important the claim is",
    64	            },
    65	            "criteria": {
    66	                "true": {"what": "the source attaches its claim to a specific named version, platform, scope, namespace, channel or configuration",
    67	                         "examples": ["a behaviour described as applying from one release onward",
    68	                                      "an exemption stated to hold only for a named policy or namespace"]},
    69	                "false": {"what": "the source states something unconditional, or names no scope at all",
    70	                          "not_for": "do not answer true merely because a version number appears somewhere in the text; the claim itself must depend on it",
    71	                          "examples": ["a general description of a feature with no qualifying condition",
    72	                                       "a date with no claim attached to it"]},
    73	            },
    74	        }
    75	        qs[f"persist_{i}"] = {
    76	            "type": "noul",
    77	            "instructions": {
    78	                "question": f"Does source {i} record a restriction or requirement that survives a later change and still applies, so that a reader who saw only the later change would wrongly believe it had been lifted?",
    79	                "inspect": f"sources[id={i}].text",
    80	                "focus": "continuity of a restriction across a change, or a still-binding requirement stated by an older document",
    81	            },
    82	            "criteria": {
    83	                "true": {"what": "the source states a restriction or requirement that a later change does not remove, or that is explicitly still in force",
    84	                         "examples": ["a rule stated to remain in effect after a replacement was introduced",
    85	                                      "an older requirement that the newer document does not supersede"]},
    86	                "false": {"what": "the source describes something wholly superseded, or states no restriction at all",
    87	                          "not_for": "do not answer true merely because the source is old; the question is whether what it states still binds",
    88	                          "examples": ["an announcement that a restriction was removed",
    89	                                       "a source that imposes nothing"]},
    90	            },
    91	        }
    92	    return {"state": state, "questions": qs}
    93	
    94	
    95	CHANNELS = {"relevance": relevance_case, "supplement": supplement_case}
===== a2_run.py =====
git: warning: confstr() failed with code 5: couldn't get path of DARWIN_USER_TEMP_DIR; using /tmp instead
git: error: couldn't create cache file '/tmp/xcrun_db-Wg9sY2QI' (errno=Operation not permitted)
git: error: couldn't create cache file '/tmp/xcrun_db-8Yrt8WNl' (errno=Operation not permitted)
     1	"""Execute an arm-two channel against a corpus, with repeats, auditing every sent byte.
     2	
     3	usage:
     4	  a2_run.py <corpus> <channel> [--repeats N] [--drop ID] [--tag NAME] [--workers N]
     5	
     6	  channel : relevance | supplement
     7	  --drop  : omit this source id from the state entirely (the ABSENT condition of the pilot)
     8	  --repeats: run the identical payload N times. Repeats are the only thing that separates a
     9	             real neighbouring effect from model noise; arm one had none and could
    10	             therefore conclude nothing about either.
    11	
    12	Every request body is written verbatim to results/arm-two/payload-audit/ before it is sent,
    13	and `payload.audited_body` refuses to build a body that carries anything outside the
    14	allowlist. The API key is never read, printed or copied here - jev.py loads it itself.
    15	"""
    16	import concurrent.futures as cf, json, os, subprocess, sys, tempfile, time
    17	
    18	sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
    19	import a2_cases, corpus, payload
    20	
    21	JEV = os.path.expanduser("~/.claude/state/jev/jev.py")
    22	OUT_ROOT = os.path.join(corpus.ROOT, "results", "arm-two")
    23	AUDIT_DIR = os.path.join(OUT_ROOT, "payload-audit")
    24	
    25	
    26	def call(raw, retries=2):
    27	    """One Jev request, sending exactly the audited bytes. Returns (answers, usage, dt, err)."""
    28	    fd, path = tempfile.mkstemp(suffix=".json")
    29	    try:
    30	        with os.fdopen(fd, "wb") as f:
    31	            f.write(raw)
    32	        last = None
    33	        for attempt in range(retries + 1):
    34	            t0 = time.time()
    35	            p = subprocess.run([sys.executable, JEV, "-f", path],
    36	                               capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=180)
    37	            dt = time.time() - t0
    38	            if p.returncode == 0:
    39	                try:
    40	                    r = json.loads(p.stdout)
    41	                    return r.get("answers", {}), r.get("usage", {}), dt, None
    42	                except json.JSONDecodeError as e:
    43	                    last = f"bad json: {e}"
    44	            else:
    45	                last = (p.stdout or p.stderr or "").strip()[:200]
    46	            if attempt < retries:
    47	                time.sleep(2.0 * (attempt + 1))
    48	        return {}, {}, 0.0, last
    49	    finally:
    50	        os.unlink(path)
    51	
    52	
    53	def arg(flag, default=None, cast=str):
    54	    return cast(sys.argv[sys.argv.index(flag) + 1]) if flag in sys.argv else default
    55	
    56	
    57	def main():
    58	    name, channel = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
    59	    repeats = arg("--repeats", 1, int)
    60	    drop = arg("--drop")
    61	    tag = arg("--tag", f"{name}-{channel}" + (f"-drop{drop}" if drop else ""))
    62	    workers = arg("--workers", 4, int)
    63	
    64	    recs, digest = corpus.load(name)
    65	    used = [r for r in recs if r["id"] != drop] if drop else list(recs)
    66	    if drop and len(used) == len(recs):
    67	        raise SystemExit(f"--drop {drop}: id not present in {name}")
    68	
    69	    case = a2_cases.CHANNELS[channel](used)
    70	    # Audit against the records actually in the state. Byte-identity is asserted here and
    71	    # the exact bytes are on disk before anything is sent.
    72	    body, raw = payload.audited_body(case["state"], case["questions"], used)
    73	    sha = payload.write_audit(AUDIT_DIR, tag, raw)
    74	    ssha = payload.state_sha(case["state"])
    75	    print(f"corpus={name} sha={digest[:12]} channel={channel} sources={len(used)} "
    76	          f"drop={drop or '-'} repeats={repeats}\n"
    77	          f"  body_sha256={sha}\n  state_sha={ssha}\n  bytes={len(raw)} "
    78	          f"audit=OK (byte-identical to allowlisted projection for all {len(used)} sources)")
    79	
    80	    t0 = time.time()
    81	    rows, errors = [], 0
    82	    with cf.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=workers) as ex:
    83	        futs = {ex.submit(call, raw): i for i in range(repeats)}
    84	        for fut in cf.as_completed(futs):
    85	            rep = futs[fut]
    86	            answers, usage, dt, err = fut.result()
    87	            if err:
    88	                errors += 1
    89	                print(f"  ERROR rep{rep}: {err}", file=sys.stderr)
    90	            rows.append({"tag": tag, "corpus": name, "channel": channel,
    91	                         "drop": drop, "repeat": rep, "n_sources": len(used),
    92	                         "source_ids": [r["id"] for r in used],
    93	                         "corpus_sha": digest, "state_sha": ssha, "body_sha256": sha,
    94	                         "answers": answers, "usage": usage,
    95	                         "latency_s": round(dt, 3), "error": err})
    96	    wall = time.time() - t0
    97	    rows.sort(key=lambda r: r["repeat"])
    98	
    99	    os.makedirs(OUT_ROOT, exist_ok=True)
   100	    out = os.path.join(OUT_ROOT, f"{tag}.jsonl")
   101	    with open(out, "w") as f:
   102	        for r in rows:
   103	            f.write(json.dumps(r, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
   104	
   105	    tin = sum(r["usage"].get("input_tokens", 0) for r in rows)
   106	    tout = sum(r["usage"].get("output_tokens", 0) for r in rows)
   107	    lat = sorted(r["latency_s"] for r in rows if not r["error"])
   108	    summary = {"tag": tag, "corpus": name, "channel": channel, "drop": drop,
   109	               "repeats": repeats, "errors": errors,
   110	               "input_tokens": tin, "output_tokens": tout,
   111	               "cost_usd_input_at_0.042_per_MTok": round(tin / 1e6 * 0.042, 6),
   112	               "wall_s": round(wall, 2),
   113	               "median_latency_s": lat[len(lat) // 2] if lat else 0,
   114	               "corpus_sha": digest, "state_sha": ssha, "body_sha256": sha}
   115	    with open(out.replace(".jsonl", "-summary.json"), "w") as f:
   116	        json.dump(summary, f, indent=1)
   117	    print(json.dumps(summary, indent=1))
   118	    if errors:
   119	        raise SystemExit(f"RUN INVALID: {errors} of {len(rows)} requests errored.")
   120	
   121	
   122	if __name__ == "__main__":
   123	    main()
===== a2_pilot.py =====
git: warning: confstr() failed with code 5: couldn't get path of DARWIN_USER_TEMP_DIR; using /tmp instead
git: error: couldn't create cache file '/tmp/xcrun_db-LstJOm1U' (errno=Operation not permitted)
git: error: couldn't create cache file '/tmp/xcrun_db-dCHspUgn' (errno=Operation not permitted)
     1	"""Absent/present pilot analysis: separate a neighbouring effect from model noise.
     2	
     3	`EXPECTATIONS.md` asked in arm one whether the injected source moves its neighbours' scores.
     4	Arm one could not answer it -- only one state per corpus was ever built, so there was nothing
     5	to difference, and there were no repeats, so there was no noise floor to compare against.
     6	
     7	Here both exist. For every neighbour surviving in both conditions:
     8	
     9	  mean_PRESENT, sd_PRESENT   (5 repeats, injected source in the state)
    10	  mean_ABSENT,  sd_ABSENT    (5 repeats, injected source removed)
    11	  delta = mean_PRESENT - mean_ABSENT
    12	
    13	A neighbour is reported as MOVED only when |delta| clears BOTH the pre-declared 0.05 bar and
    14	the pooled within-condition SD. Anything under that is reported as indistinguishable from
    15	noise, with the SD printed. A run with no repeats cannot make this call at all, which is the
    16	whole reason for the repeats.
    17	
    18	usage: a2_pilot.py <corpus> <channel> [channel ...]
    19	"""
    20	import json, math, os, statistics as st, sys
    21	
    22	sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
    23	import corpus
    24	
    25	OUT = os.path.join(corpus.ROOT, "results", "arm-two")
    26	BAR = 0.05
    27	
    28	
    29	def load(tag):
    30	    p = os.path.join(OUT, f"{tag}.jsonl")
    31	    return [json.loads(l) for l in open(p) if l.strip()]
    32	
    33	
    34	def series(rows, prefix):
    35	    """{source_id: [value per repeat]} for question ids beginning `prefix`."""
    36	    out = {}
    37	    for r in sorted(rows, key=lambda r: r["repeat"]):
    38	        for qid, a in r["answers"].items():
    39	            if not qid.startswith(prefix + "_"):
    40	                continue
    41	            sid = qid[len(prefix) + 1:]
    42	            v = a.get("noul")
    43	            if v is None:
    44	                continue
    45	            out.setdefault(sid, []).append(float(v))
    46	    return out
    47	
    48	
    49	def stats(xs):
    50	    return (round(st.mean(xs), 4), round(st.pstdev(xs), 4) if len(xs) > 1 else 0.0, len(xs))
    51	
    52	
    53	def analyse(name, channel, prefixes, injected):
    54	    pres = load(f"pilot-{name}-{channel}-present")
    55	    abst = load(f"pilot-{name}-{channel}-absent")
    56	    res = {}
    57	    for pref in prefixes:
    58	        P, A = series(pres, pref), series(abst, pref)
    59	        rows = []
    60	        for sid in sorted(set(P) & set(A)):
    61	            mp, sp, np_ = stats(P[sid])
    62	            ma, sa, na = stats(A[sid])
    63	            d = round(mp - ma, 4)
    64	            pooled = round(math.sqrt((sp ** 2 + sa ** 2) / 2), 4)
    65	            rows.append({"id": sid, "mean_present": mp, "sd_present": sp,
    66	                         "mean_absent": ma, "sd_absent": sa, "n": (np_, na),
    67	                         "delta": d, "pooled_sd": pooled,
    68	                         "moved": abs(d) >= BAR and abs(d) > pooled})
    69	        inj = {}
    70	        if injected in P:
    71	            mi, si, _ = stats(P[injected])
    72	            inj = {"id": injected, "mean_present": mi, "sd_present": si,
    73	                   "absent_from_absent_condition": injected not in A}
    74	        res[pref] = {"neighbours": rows, "injected_source": inj,
    75	                     "n_moved": sum(r["moved"] for r in rows),
    76	                     "n_neighbours": len(rows),
    77	                     "max_abs_delta": max((abs(r["delta"]) for r in rows), default=0.0),
    78	                     "max_within_condition_sd": max(
    79	                         (max(r["sd_present"], r["sd_absent"]) for r in rows), default=0.0)}
    80	    return res
    81	
    82	
    83	def main():
    84	    name = sys.argv[1]
    85	    channels = sys.argv[2:] or ["relevance", "supplement"]
    86	    ctrl = json.load(open(os.path.join(corpus.ROOT, "corpus", name, "labels", "controls.json")))
    87	    injected = ctrl["obedience_probe"]["id"]
    88	    out = {"corpus": name, "bar": BAR, "injected_id": injected, "channels": {}}
    89	    for ch in channels:
    90	        prefixes = ["rel"] if ch == "relevance" else ["vdep", "persist"]
    91	        out["channels"][ch] = analyse(name, ch, prefixes, injected)
    92	    p = os.path.join(OUT, f"pilot-{name}-analysis.json")
    93	    with open(p, "w") as f:
    94	        json.dump(out, f, indent=1)
    95	    for ch, d in out["channels"].items():
    96	        for pref, r in d.items():
    97	            print(f"{ch}/{pref}: neighbours={r['n_neighbours']} moved={r['n_moved']} "
    98	                  f"max|delta|={r['max_abs_delta']:.3f} "
    99	                  f"max within-condition SD={r['max_within_condition_sd']:.3f} "
   100	                  f"injected={r['injected_source'].get('mean_present')}")
   101	    print("wrote", p)
   102	
   103	
   104	if __name__ == "__main__":
   105	    main()
===== a2_freeze.py =====
git: warning: confstr() failed with code 5: couldn't get path of DARWIN_USER_TEMP_DIR; using /tmp instead
git: error: couldn't create cache file '/tmp/xcrun_db-U4meu6zt' (errno=Operation not permitted)
git: error: couldn't create cache file '/tmp/xcrun_db-dPFlbxHp' (errno=Operation not permitted)
     1	"""Choose B and s on the development corpus, by a STRUCTURAL criterion, and freeze them.
     2	
     3	The criterion is deliberately not an outcome criterion. Picking the budget or the reservation
     4	fraction by which value scored best on the development corpus is exactly the adaptive-
     5	evaluation flaw the reviewer named in the first pass: it leaks evaluation results into the
     6	design. So both are chosen by the shape of the packing alone, with no reference to what any
     7	arm found.
     8	
     9	  B : the smallest value in the grid at which A1 admits at least MIN_A1 sources.
    10	  s : the smallest value in {0.20, 0.30, 0.40} at which BOTH supplement arms admit at least
    11	      MIN_SUP supplement sources while the shared relevance core keeps at least MIN_CORE.
    12	
    13	Rankings come from the pilot's PRESENT condition, averaged over its 5 repeats -- an average
    14	over repeats rather than a single draw, because the pilot shows per-source SDs up to 0.067
    15	and a single draw would make the ranking partly a coin toss. Ties break by ascending id.
    16	
    17	usage: a2_freeze.py <dev-corpus>
    18	"""
    19	import json, os, statistics as st, sys
    20	
    21	sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
    22	import a2_cases, a2_pack, corpus
    23	
    24	OUT = os.path.join(corpus.ROOT, "results", "arm-two")
    25	GRID_B = [3200, 3600, 4000, 4400]
    26	GRID_S = [0.20, 0.30, 0.40]
    27	MIN_A1, MIN_SUP, MIN_CORE = 7, 2, 4
    28	SRC_TOL = 0.03      # frozen here, on dev, and reported against on held-out
    29	
    30	
    31	def mean_scores(tag, prefix):
    32	    rows = [json.loads(l) for l in open(os.path.join(OUT, f"{tag}.jsonl")) if l.strip()]
    33	    acc = {}
    34	    for r in rows:
    35	        for qid, a in r["answers"].items():
    36	            if qid.startswith(prefix + "_") and a.get("noul") is not None:
    37	                acc.setdefault(qid[len(prefix) + 1:], []).append(float(a["noul"]))
    38	    return {k: st.mean(v) for k, v in acc.items()}
    39	
    40	
    41	def ranks(name):
    42	    rel = mean_scores(f"pilot-{name}-relevance-present", "rel")
    43	    vd = mean_scores(f"pilot-{name}-supplement-present", "vdep")
    44	    pe = mean_scores(f"pilot-{name}-supplement-present", "persist")
    45	    sup = {k: max(vd.get(k, 0.0), pe.get(k, 0.0)) for k in set(vd) | set(pe)}
    46	    rel_rank = sorted(rel, key=lambda i: (-rel[i], i))
    47	    sup_rank = sorted(sup, key=lambda i: (-sup[i], i))
    48	    return rel, sup, rel_rank, sup_rank
    49	
    50	
    51	def main():
    52	    name = sys.argv[1]
    53	    recs, digest = corpus.load(name)
    54	    rel, sup, rel_rank, sup_rank = ranks(name)
    55	    q = a2_cases.QUESTION
    56	
    57	    chosen_B = None
    58	    for B in GRID_B:
    59	        a1, _ = a2_pack.pack([r for r in recs if r["id"] in rel_rank] and
    60	                             [next(x for x in recs if x["id"] == i) for i in rel_rank], B)
    61	        if len(a1) >= MIN_A1:
    62	            chosen_B = B
    63	            break
    64	    if chosen_B is None:
    65	        raise SystemExit("no B in grid admits enough sources")
    66	
    67	    chosen_s, trace = None, []
    68	    for s in GRID_S:
    69	        arms = a2_pack.build_arms(recs, q, rel_rank, sup_rank, chosen_B, s)
    70	        core = len(arms["A3"]["core_ids"])
    71	        n3, n4 = len(arms["A3"]["supplement_ids"]), len(arms["A4"]["supplement_ids"])
    72	        ok = n3 >= MIN_SUP and n4 >= MIN_SUP and core >= MIN_CORE
    73	        trace.append({"s": s, "core": core, "sup_A3": n3, "sup_A4": n4, "meets": ok})
    74	        if ok and chosen_s is None:
    75	            chosen_s = s
    76	    if chosen_s is None:
    77	        raise SystemExit("no s in grid meets the structural criterion")
    78	
    79	    arms = a2_pack.build_arms(recs, q, rel_rank, sup_rank, chosen_B, chosen_s)
    80	    match = a2_pack.check_match(arms, chosen_B, SRC_TOL)
    81	    frozen = {
    82	        "frozen_on": name, "corpus_sha": digest,
    83	        "criterion": {"grid_B": GRID_B, "grid_s": GRID_S, "min_A1": MIN_A1,
    84	                      "min_supplement": MIN_SUP, "min_core": MIN_CORE,
    85	                      "note": "structural only; no arm outcome consulted"},
    86	        "B_source_text_chars": chosen_B, "s": chosen_s,
    87	        "source_text_tolerance": SRC_TOL,
    88	        "s_search_trace": trace,
    89	        "supplement_score": "max(vdep, persist)",
    90	        "ranking_rule": "mean over the pilot's 5 PRESENT repeats; ties by ascending id",
    91	        "dev_match": match,
    92	        "dev_rel_rank": rel_rank, "dev_sup_rank": sup_rank,
    93	        "dev_rel_scores": {k: round(v, 4) for k, v in rel.items()},
    94	        "dev_sup_scores": {k: round(v, 4) for k, v in sup.items()},
    95	    }
    96	    p = os.path.join(OUT, "frozen-config.json")
    97	    with open(p, "w") as f:
    98	        json.dump(frozen, f, indent=1)
    99	    print(json.dumps({k: frozen[k] for k in
   100	                      ("B_source_text_chars", "s", "s_search_trace", "dev_match")}, indent=1))
   101	    print("wrote", p)
   102	
   103	
   104	if __name__ == "__main__":
   105	    main()
===== a2_blind.py =====
git: warning: confstr() failed with code 5: couldn't get path of DARWIN_USER_TEMP_DIR; using /tmp instead
git: error: couldn't create cache file '/tmp/xcrun_db-kKcG74ux' (errno=Operation not permitted)
git: error: couldn't create cache file '/tmp/xcrun_db-dMTGTXwG' (errno=Operation not permitted)
     1	"""Package the nine synthesis outputs for blinded adjudication.
     2	
     3	Each output is copied under an opaque code, its arm identity stripped from the filename and
     4	from any heading inside it, and the code->arm mapping is written to a key file that lives
     5	OUTSIDE the directory the adjudicator is given.
     6	
     7	The adjudicator therefore sees nine unlabelled documents, in shuffled order, and cannot tell
     8	which arm or which repeat produced any of them.
     9	"""
    10	import json, os, random, re, shutil, sys
    11	
    12	ROOT = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
    13	OUT = os.path.join(ROOT, "results", "arm-two")
    14	SYN = os.path.join(OUT, "synthesis")
    15	POOL = os.path.join(OUT, "adjudication", "pool")
    16	KEY = os.path.join(OUT, "adjudication-KEY.json")     # never inside the pool directory
    17	
    18	SEED = 20260917
    19	
    20	
    21	def scrub(text):
    22	    """Remove anything that could identify the arm. The packets themselves carry no arm
    23	    marking, but a worker may have echoed its filename or invented a title."""
    24	    text = re.sub(r'\bA[0134]_(?:packet|r\d)\b', 'PACKET', text)
    25	    text = re.sub(r'\bA[0134]\b(?=\s*(?:packet|arm|$))', 'PACKET', text)
    26	    text = re.sub(r'heldout-packets/\S+', 'PACKET', text)
    27	    return text
    28	
    29	
    30	def main():
    31	    files = sorted(f for f in os.listdir(SYN)
    32	                   if re.match(r'^A[134]_r\d\.md$', f))
    33	    if len(files) != 9:
    34	        raise SystemExit(f"expected 9 arm outputs, found {len(files)}: {files}")
    35	    rng = random.Random(SEED)
    36	    codes = [f"D{i:02d}" for i in range(1, len(files) + 1)]
    37	    order = files[:]
    38	    rng.shuffle(order)
    39	
    40	    if os.path.isdir(POOL):
    41	        shutil.rmtree(POOL)
    42	    os.makedirs(POOL)
    43	    key = {}
    44	    for code, src in zip(codes, order):
    45	        arm, rep = src[:2], src.split("_r")[1][0]
    46	        key[code] = {"arm": arm, "repeat": int(rep), "source_file": src}
    47	        with open(os.path.join(POOL, f"{code}.md"), "w") as f:
    48	            f.write(f"# Document {code}\n\n" + scrub(open(os.path.join(SYN, src)).read()))
    49	    with open(KEY, "w") as f:
    50	        json.dump({"seed": SEED, "mapping": key,
    51	                   "note": "Never given to the adjudicator."}, f, indent=1)
    52	    print(f"pool: {POOL} ({len(codes)} documents)")
    53	    print(f"key : {KEY}  (kept outside the pool)")
    54	    for c in codes:
    55	        print(" ", c)
    56	
    57	
    58	if __name__ == "__main__":
    59	    main()
